# RANGE: F139-L11..F141-L02 (the anchors AK05-... of ../anchors.tsv), or one anchor. In the text: [^n] a translator's
# note, whose text is the next "@n" line after the block; {ar:...} words in Arabic letters; ⟨ ⟩ what Mžik supplies.

@p F048-L01..F048-L06
The description of the mountain that surrounds the Island of the Ruby, which lies at the beginning of the boundary of the east, in the farthest part of the Sea of China, ⟨which is⟩ the Dark Sea, on which no one sails. A mountain surrounding the Island of the Ruby begins at longitude 167° 0′ and latitude 14° 30′ (596) [1457], passing to latitude 9° 0′ in a straight line (597) [1458]; then it turns round to latitude 5° 0′ and longitude 170° 0′ (598) [1459]; it passes on and enters the Dark Sea at latitude 4° 0′ and longitude 172° 0′ (599) [1460], and enters the sea at latitude 8° 30′ and longitude 178° 30′ (600) [1461]; then it surrounds the island in the shape of a basket; then the mountain comes.

@p F049-L01..F049-L06
Along longitude 178° 30′ until it ends at the equator (601) [1462]; then it turns round to longitude 176° 30′, the latitude being 3° 15/55′ beyond the equator (602) [1463]; then it turns round to longitude 179° 0′, touching the equator (603) [1464]; then it passes along this longitude until the latitude, in the first clime, is 6° 0′ (604) [1465]; then it turns round until the latitude is 9° 30′ and the longitude 176° 30′ (605) [1466]; then it turns round until the longitude is 178° 0′ and the latitude 5° 30′ (606) [1467]. Its colour is red; it is eastern, the mountain begins towards the east and passes round the island, and this is its figure.

@p F112-L05..F114-L01
⟨The Nile of Egypt and the springs and the rivers that fall into it⟩.

Two round marshes, the diameter of each five degrees: the centre of the first is at longitude 50° 0′ and latitude 7° 0′ (1607) [1503], and the centre of the second at longitude 57° 0′ and latitude 7° 0′ (1608) [1504].

Into the first there pour five rivers from the Mountain of the Moon: the beginning of the first river is at longitude 48° 0′ (1609) [1505], the second at ⟨longitude⟩ 49° 0′ (1610) [1506], the third at longitude 50° 0′ (1611) [1507], the fourth at longitude 51° 0′ (1612) [1508] and the fifth at longitude 52° 0′ (1613) [1509].

From the Mountain of the Moon there also come out five rivers to the second marsh: the beginning of the first river is at longitude 55° 20′ (1614) [1510], the second at 56° 20′ (1615) [1511], the third at 57° 20′ (1616) [1512], the fourth at 58° 20′ (1617) [1513] and the fifth at 59° 20′ (1618) [1514].

From these two marshes there come out, from each of them, four rivers to a round marsh in the first clime; the diameter of this marsh is two degrees, and its centre is at longitude 58° 30′ and latitude, in the first clime, 2° 0′ (1619) [1515].

The beginning of the first river from the first marsh is at longitude 48° 40′ (1620) [1516], the second at longitude 49° 30′ (1621) [1517] and the third at longitude 51° 15′ (1622) [1518]; the second and the third join at longitude 52° 0′ and latitude 1° 0′ beyond the equator (1623) [1519]; then it passes to the marsh that we have mentioned as one river; and the fourth river is at longitude 52° 0′ (1624) [1520].

And of the rivers that come from the second marsh to the small one, the beginning of the first is likewise at longitude 55° 30′ (1625) [1521], the second at longitude 56° 20′ (1626) [1522] and the third at ⟨longitude⟩ 58° 0′ (1627) [1523]; the second and the third meet at longitude 56° 0′ and latitude 1° 0′ beyond the equator (1628) [1524]; then it passes to the marsh that we have mentioned as one river; and the fourth river is at longitude 59° 0′ (1629) [1525]. All these rivers come to the small marsh, the mouth of each not touching the other.

Then ⟨there comes out of this small marsh a great river, which is the Nile⟩ of Egypt. It passes by the land of the Blacks, ⟨ʿAlwa and Zaghāwa⟩, Fazzān and Nubia, and passes by Dunqula, the city of Nubia, at longitude 52° 20′ and latitude 2° 0′ from the first clime, counted from beyond it towards the equator, or, from the equator to it, 15° 0′ (1630) [1526]. The Nile passes on and cuts the line of the first clime at longitude 53° 0′ and latitude 16° 20′ (1631) [1527], and it passes on until it goes beyond the line of the.

First clime by a degree and a half in its direction (1632) [1528]; then it turns aside to longitude 52° 0′ and latitude 18° 40′ (1633) [1529]; then it turns aside to longitude 51° 0′ and latitude 17° 0′ (1634) [1530]; then it turns aside to longitude 50° 0′ and latitude 17° 30′ (1635) [1531]; then it turns aside to longitude 50° 20′ and latitude 18° 20′ (1636) [1532]; then it turns aside to longitude 52° 30′ and latitude 19° 40′ (1637) [1533]; then it passes and comes to the city of {ar:ملوى} at longitude 51° 30′ and latitude 19° 20′ (1638) [1534]; then it passes and comes to the city of Aswān, touching it, at longitude 55° 30′ and latitude 22° 30′ (1639) [1535]; then it turns aside ⟨to longitude⟩ 58° 0′ and latitude 24° 0′ (1640) [1536], touching the mountain {ar:اليوڡا}; then it turns aside to longitude 55° 30′ and latitude 24° 20′ (1641) [1537]; then it passes and falls in the third clime, touching in some places the mountain al-Muqaṭṭam and the cities on it, whose longitude and latitude we have written down; then it passes to Miṣr, touching it, at longitude 54° 30′ and latitude 29° 15′ (1642) [1538]; then it branches off from Miṣr to the sea in seven channels.

⟨This⟩ marsh: its beginning is from longitude 58° ⟨45′⟩ (2379) [1035] ⟨to longitude⟩ ⟨59°⟩ 40′ (2380) [1036]. It begins from the place of the channel of the four rivers, in the measure of the breadth of the four ⟨rivers . . . . . .⟩ the two sides, until it comes to that one of its sides which is next to the sea, at longitude 68° 30′ ⟨and latitude 52° 30′⟩ (2381); ⟨then⟩ the marsh ⟨turns⟩ and passes by the city of {ar:طرمى}, and passes ⟨by the five rivers⟩ whose mouth is at longitude 69° 0′ (2382) and whose beginning is from the mountain {ar:افقا}, until it reaches the corner of the city of ⟨{ar:طاس}⟩; then it passes to a place whose longitude is 68° 40′ and latitude 57° 40′ (2383), and passes to a place whose longitude is 62° ⟨0′⟩ and latitude 55° 30′ (2384) [1037]; then it passes to a place below the channel of the four rivers. Then two rivers come out of it between the two seas, and their mouths are in the Sea of the North. And this is the picture of that.
@p F163-L08..F163-L14
⟨The river {ar:را}: its beginning is from a mountain at longitude 72° 20′ and latitude 60° 30′⟩ (2385) [2212]; ⟨it passes to a place⟩ whose longitude is 81° 30′ and latitude ⟨58° 30′⟩ (2386) [2213]; and passes to a place whose longitude is 75° 40′ and latitude ⟨57°⟩ ⟨20′⟩ (2387) [2214]; ⟨and passes and strikes⟩ one of the two towers ⟨that⟩ Dhū l-Qarnayn ⟨built⟩, and passes on and flows into the sea at longitude ⟨81°⟩ 10′ and latitude 49° 20′ (2388) [2215].

⟨And there falls into⟩ this river a river from a mountain, its beginning is at longitude 92° 30′ and latitude 61° 0′ (2389) [2216], and its mouth is in the river at longitude 81° 30′ and latitude 58° 30′ (2390) [2217].
@p F164-L01..F164-L06
Two rivers from the mountain {ar:ڡرمانيا}: the beginning of one of them is at longitude ⟨78°⟩ ⟨0′⟩ and latitude 51° 0′ (2391) [2218], and its mouth is in the sea at longitude 79° 30′ and latitude 48° 30′ (2392) [2219]; and the source of the second is at longitude 78° 40′ and latitude 51° 30′ (2393) [2220], and its mouth is in the sea at longitude 80° 40′ and latitude ⟨49°⟩ ⟨0′⟩ (2394) [2221].

A river from the mountain {ar:روميقا}, its beginning is at longitude 88° 0′ and latitude 57° 0′ (2395) [2222], and its mouth is in ⟨the sea⟩ at longitude 84° 0′ and latitude 50° 20′ (2396) [2223].
@p F164-L07..F164-L12
A river from the mountain {ar:زوميڡا}, its beginning is at longitude 89° 0′ and latitude 56° 45′ (2397) [2224], and its mouth is in the sea at longitude 85° 30′ and latitude 51° 0′ (2398) [2225].

There falls into it a river from the mountain {ar:روميڡا}, its beginning is at longitude 91° 0′ and latitude 56° 5′ (2399) [2226], and its mouth is in the river at longitude 88° 0′ and latitude 54° 0′ (2400) [2227].

And there falls into the first river also a river from the mountain {ar:ارساٮا}, its beginning is at longitude 97° 0′ and latitude 56° 0′ (2401) [2228], and its mouth is in the first river at longitude 87° 20′ and latitude 58° 0′ (2402) [2229].
